[^jcf3-en-i]: The present value normalizes the costs that occur over time by inspecting them in today's dollars.

[^jcf3-en-ii]: Reliability means that the conduit will hold up and not be the cause of electrical system outages. The reduction in outages because of the conduit improvements are estimated as part of the evaluation, along with an estimate of the financial value related to the reduced outages.

[^jcf3-en-iii]: The MD PSC's questions are posed in its December 14, 2023, Order No. 90948 in Case No. 9692. The Order directs BGE to address requirements identified by the MD PSC Staff Witness Samrawit Dererie in direct testimony dated June 20, 2023 [source shows a stray "{date}" token immediately after "2023." here; likely a template placeholder left unresolved in the source document [sic]]. The call out box paraphrases the questions in a manner that West Monroe and BGE believes is well aligned to the MD PSC's request. The MD PSC has also sought perspective on how 2023 Agreement conduit benefits and costs may relate or be attributable to other conduit users/tenants. BGE acknowledges this but will address this question outside of this BCA report.

[^jcf3-en-iv]: The estimate of the marginal difference, and the ratio, are based on taking the estimate of costs and benefits over the forecast period and "rolling them up" into their present value.

[^jcf3-en-v]: As a technical financial matter, the old agreement costs are $319 million over the 7-year term. This compares to $235 million for the new agreement over the same period. When these values are applied to the method of creating utility bills (for that portion that is collected through the BGE customer), these values translate into approximately $274 million each, meaning there is limited difference in present value cost between the two agreement forms.

#### Operating Income Adjustment 20E: Amortize Electric Major Outage Event Expenses

_For the Twelve Months Ending March 31, 2026_

This adjustment reflects the amortization associated with the two major outage events that occurred in August 2023 and February 2025. The incremental expenses associated with each event were deferred to a regulatory asset and are currently included in unadjusted rate base. In Case No. 9645, Order No. 89678, the Commission granted BGE's request to create a regulatory asset for major outage event expenses as it aligns recovery of major outage events with the actual incremental costs to restore service. Therefore, the Company is requesting recovery of these costs over a five-year period consistent with Commission Order No. 89678. Rate Base Adjustment 7E is the companion adjustment.

#### Operating Income Adjustment 23E: Adjust Tax Expense for NOLC Impact

_For the Twelve Months Ending March 31, 2026_

This adjustment provides for increased tax expense associated with certain restatements required to align with IRS guidance addressing net operating loss carryforwards (NOLCs). Regulated utilities participating in tax sharing arrangements are required to report regulated operating income and regulated rate base as if the tax sharing had not occurred in order to avoid a normalization violation. This operating income adjusts current tax expense in compliance with these requirements. The companion rate base adjustment is Rate Base Adjustment 10E.

| Impact of NOLC Restatement on Tax Expense  | $1,547,249   |

This adjustment reduces amortization expense as a result of House Bill 864 from the 2024 General Assembly session and Order No. 91175 in Case No. 9075 [Note: this order/case pairing echoes the reference in Adjustment 25E below, which cites "Case No. 9705"; the two case numbers appear inconsistently across these two adjustment descriptions in the source. [sic]], which limited the return on the remaining regulatory asset balance for EmPOWER Maryland programs to the utility weighted average cost of debt, effective July 1, 2024. #### Operating Income Adjustment 25E: To Reflect Revised Cap Structure

_For the Twelve Months Ending March 31, 2026_

This adjustment adjusts the electric distribution capital structure for HB 864 and Order No. 91175 in Case No. 9705. As a result of HB 864 and Order No. 91175, the return on the remaining EmPOWER Maryland regulatory asset is limited to BGE's average cost of debt. As such, this operating income adjustment serves to adjust the rate of return for the amount of debt available to finance electric distribution rate base exclusive of EmPOWER Maryland, which is reduced due to Order No. 91175. In other words, if the EmPOWER Maryland regulatory asset is assumed to be financed with only debt, that debt is no longer available to finance the rest of distribution rate base, and this adjustment applies the resultant higher rate of return to rate base excluding EmPOWER Maryland.

| Adjusted Rate Base                                                 | $6,055,793,738 |
| Less EmPOWER Maryland Average Rate Base Receiving Debt Only Return | (257,768,804)  |
| Adjusted Rate Base Less EmPOWER MD                                 | $5,798,024,933 |
| Change in Rate of Return Needed                                    | 0.12%          |
| Equity Return to be Eliminated                                     | $6,957,630     |
| Income Tax Effect at 27.5175%                                      | (1,914,566)    |
| Adjustment to Operating Income                                     | 5,043,064      |

#### Operating Income Adjustment 31E: Non-labor O&M General Inflation

_For the Twelve Months Ending March 31, 2026_

This adjustment reduces operating income to provide for the effect of general inflation on non-labor O&M costs during the rate-effective period. This adjustment was authorized by the Commission in Case No. 9484 to help address regulatory lag given that historical test years do not reflect systemic inflation. This adjustment reflects a 4.64% inflation factor based on a five-year average of the CPI per the U.S. Department of Labor, Bureau of Labor Statistics, as authorized by the Commission in Case No. 9484, Order No. 88975.

#### Operating Income Adjustment 32E: Prepaid Service Program O&M Recovery

_For the Twelve Months Ending March 31, 2026_

This adjustment provides for recovery of O&M related to the prepaid service program (BGE FlexPay). Under this program which allows customers greater control over the affordability of their energy bills, customers can choose to prepay for their anticipated energy consumption, and through regular communication from BGE and self-service digital tools, can track how their usage is trending in relation to their pre-payment and add to their prepaid account as needed. This adjustment provides for the O&M expense to be incurred in the rate-effective period to implement this program.

#### Operating Income Adjustment 33E: Reflect Five-Year Avg for Major Outage Events

_For the Twelve Months Ended March 31, 2026_

This adjustment reflects a level of incremental major outage event restoration O&M expense based on a five-year weighted average of these expenses. This adjustment provides for a level of incremental O&M major outage event restoration expense which serves as a base level proxy for major storm expenses in operating income for the rate-effective period. To the extent that the Company incurs more or less storm expense than the level provided in this adjustment, any difference will be recovered – or passed back to customers through the proposed Storm Restoration Expense Rider.

#### Rate Base Adjustment 11E: Eliminate CAMT Impact from Rate Base

_As of March 31, 2026_

This adjustment removes the impact of the Corporate Alternative Minimum Tax ("CAMT") from the Company's rate base for the entire twelve-month period. In February 2026, the IRS issued Notice 2026-7 which permits eligible taxpayers to reduce their Adjusted Financial Statement Income by taking regular tax deductions for repairs on Section 168 property. The noted IRS guidance effectively negates the impact of CAMT for BGE effective February 2026. This rate base adjustment removes the impact of CAMT from BGE's accumulated deferred income tax accounts for the entire test year of this rate case filing.

#### Rate Base Adjustment 13E: Establishes SCB System Costs Regulatory Asset in Rate Base

_As of March 31, 2026_

This adjustment establishes a second new SCB system costs regulatory asset for costs associated with the development of system capabilities to accommodate supplier consolidated billing. COMAR 20.53.05.07, COMAR 20.59.05.09, and Case No. 9461, Order No. 89116 require Maryland utilities to offer SCB to third-party energy providers. After incurring significant development costs, Senate Bill 1 ("SB 1") was enacted during the 2024 General Assembly session which significantly changed the residential competitive energy markets in Maryland. On July 12, 2024, the Commission issued Order No. 91220 which denied the request of the Exelon Utilities to pause continued work on SCB implementation in light of the SB 1 legislation. Subsequently, on December 11, 2024, in Case No. 9461, the Commission issued Order No. 91448 which directed utilities to "pause any new SCB implementation work until such time as retail suppliers notify the Commission of their intent to participate in the testing of SCB and their ongoing commitment to actively participate in SCB." Costs associated with these regulatory-mandated development activities for the purpose of SCB implementation are currently included in CWIP and are reflected in rate base. Given that these investments will never be placed in service and as a result, depreciation and related cost recovery as capital will never commence, the Company proposes to move these costs out of CWIP and into a regulatory asset account which will facilitate amortization and recovery in rates as the Company is seeking to recover these costs over a five-year period.

# Company Exhibit JCF-7: Electric Distribution Plant Additions – Summary by Category and Project[^jcf7-fn1]

**Period covered**: January 2024 – March 2026

This exhibit itemizes BGE's electric distribution capital additions placed in service (or under construction, for CWIP) during the test period, organized by category and project. For each project at or above the reporting threshold, the exhibit provides the in-service date, dollar amount, and a **Problem Statement**, **Solution Statement**, and **Benefits Statement**. Projects below the threshold[^jcf7-fn2] are aggregated into a single "Total Projects < Threshold" line per category, without individual narrative. Projects marked with an asterisk (*) have a customer contribution in aid of construction ("CIAC") component.

Given the scale of this exhibit (24 pages, 21 categories, 100 itemized projects plus 19 below-threshold aggregate lines), it is presented below in two parts: (1) a summary table of category totals by sponsoring witness, matching the exhibit's own subtotal structure, and (2) the full itemized detail for every project, organized by sponsor and category, preserving every Problem/Solution/Benefits narrative verbatim. A literal wide pipe-table replicating the PDF's six-column layout would render as an unreadable wall of text given the paragraph-length narrative cells; the structured list format below preserves 100% of the exhibit's content (every category, every project, every narrative sentence, every dollar figure) while remaining legible.

## Itemized project detail

### Witness Wright – sponsored categories

**Category: Capacity Planning and Management - Distribution** — Category Total: \$89,472,564

- **59335: Rock Ridge 33833 Reconductoring** — In-Service Date: June 2024 — Amount: \$7,356,250
  - _Problem Statement_: This project is required to address a 34kV contingency overload at winter peak conditions. The loss of another feeder will cause this feeder to exceed its emergency capacity. This scenario puts the three substations that serve seven feeders, and approximately 8,800 customers, including schools and critical infrastructure, at risk for extended outages.
  - _Solution Statement_: Reconductor the feeder to increase capacity and prevent the potential overload.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project eliminates a 34kV contingency overload risk that could otherwise expose approximately 8,800 customers, including schools and critical infrastructure, to extended outages. By increasing feeder capacity, replacing aging equipment, and reducing future maintenance needs, the project helps ensure continued safe and reliable electric service to over 8,000 customers, including schools and critical infrastructure.
- **59398: Clare Street 34kV Substation Build** — In-Service Date: October 2024 — Amount: \$42,741,134
  - _Problem Statement_: Built in the 1930s, Westport Substation is one of the oldest substations on the BGE system. Infrastructure at the Westport 34kV Substation is aging. Construction of a new substation at the Clare Street site is required to replace the Westport Substation to support existing customer loads and projected new customer loads in the area.
  - _Solution Statement_: Build Clare Street 34kV Substation on property adjacent to Westport 34kV Substation. This project is related to capital Project 59399.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves system reliability and operational performance by replacing aging Westport substation infrastructure with a new Clare Street 34kV substation that supports existing and future load growth. It also reduces corrective maintenance costs, and addresses protection and operational concerns.
- **59399: Clare Street Substation 34kV Feeders** — In-Service Date: April 2025 — Amount: \$7,677,397
  - _Problem Statement_: Aging infrastructure at the Westport 34kV Substation is a concern and construction of a new substation is required to support existing customer loads and projected new customer loads in the area. Once the new substation is built at Clare Street, the existing 34kV load will need to be transferred from the Westport Substation.
  - _Solution Statement_: Supply the 34kV feeders currently served by the aging Westport Substation via the newly constructed Clare Street 34kV substation being built under capital Project 59398.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves system reliability and operational performance by replacing aging Westport substation infrastructure with a new Clare Street 34kV substation that supports existing and future load growth. It also reduces corrective maintenance costs, addresses protection and operational concerns.
- **67856: Riverside New Transformer 110-5 (13kV)** — In-Service Date: November 2024 — Amount: \$5,083,031
  - _Problem Statement_: There is only one 13kV transformer at this substation, which presents a single point of failure, and as a result there are limitations on overall reliability and load expansion.
  - _Solution Statement_: Install a second 13kV transformer to support reliability, enhance operational flexibility, and increase capacity.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports reliability and operational flexibility at Riverside Substation by adding a second 13kV transformer, eliminating the existing single point of failure and reducing the risk of sustained outages. It also increases station capacity, supports future load growth, reduces maintenance costs, and enables the long-term strategic rebuild of the station.
- **87938: East Towson Substation Rebuild Land** — In-Service Date: December 2025 — Amount: \$10,499,190
  - _Problem Statement_: Land is required to rebuild a substation in the East Towson area, where appropriate real estate is scarce. Substation is needed due to forecasted capacity constraints. Land needs to be purchased first.
  - _Solution Statement_: Purchase new land in order to rebuild 115kV to 13kV Substation to satisfy forecasted capacity constraints.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Purchase of new land allows the future rebuild of the 115kV to 13kV East Towson Substation, as the current site does not have enough land to support the rebuild.
- _Total Projects < Threshold[^jcf7-fn2]_ — Amount: \$16,115,561

**Category: Conduit** — Category Total: \$80,497,005

- **87472: Conduit Capital** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$80,497,005
  - _Problem Statement_: The Baltimore City conduit system is outmoded, causing disruptive and costly outages and damage. The conduit requires infrastructure investments in accordance with the amended agreement executed between the City of Baltimore and BGE in 2023.
  - _Solution Statement_: Systematically replace outmoded materials with more modern materials, which have a higher resistance to impact, higher failure rating, and can withstand significant stress from environmental factors.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Infrastructure investments in the Baltimore City conduit system help ensure safe and reliable electric service to BGE's customers and fulfill BGE's obligation under the 2023 amended agreement with the City of Baltimore, while allowing BGE to prioritize projects for its customers.

**Category: Corrective Maintenance - Distribution** — Category Total: \$187,000,192

- **60472: Buried Primary Cable Main Faults** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$8,428,386
  - _Problem Statement_: A fault occurs on primary cable mains causing an outage.
  - _Solution Statement_: Cable fault is located, excavated, and defective cable is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by locating and excavating faults on primary cable mains and replacing the defective cable that is causing the outage.
- **60473: Buried Primary Cable Tap Faults** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$13,292,975
  - _Problem Statement_: A fault occurs on primary cable tap causing an outage.
  - _Solution Statement_: Cable fault is located, excavated, and a small section of the defective cable is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by locating and excavating cable faults on primary cable taps and replacing the defective cable that is causing the outage.
- **60475: Buried Secondary Cable Residential Faults** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$6,732,123
  - _Problem Statement_: A fault occurs on secondary (lower voltage) cable causing an outage to residential customers.
  - _Solution Statement_: Cable fault is located, excavated, and a small section of the defective cable is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by locating and excavating faults on secondary cable and replacing the defective cable that is causing the outage.
- **60477: Pad-Mounted Distribution Transformer Replacements - Planned** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,406,363
  - _Problem Statement_: A pad-mounted distribution transformer is identified as beyond repair through an inspection program required under COMAR 20.50.12.10 or due to an expected failure and requires replacement.
  - _Solution Statement_: Pad-mounted transformer is replaced. Cost for the transformer unit allocated to this program as they are installed from stock.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by replacing pad-mounted distribution transformers identified as beyond repair through inspection or due to expected failure.
- **60481: Switchgear Replacement** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,790,851
  - _Problem Statement_: A switchgear or module is identified as beyond repair through inspection program required under COMAR 20.50.12.10 and requires replacement.
  - _Solution Statement_: Switchgear or module is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by replacing switchgear or modules identified through inspection as beyond repair.
- **60487: Distribution Line Clearance Program - Capital** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$4,995,076
  - _Problem Statement_: Line clearance deficiencies are identified on BGE's overhead distribution system. Deficiencies are those conditions where overhead line clearances do not meet BGE's Overhead Construction Standards and/or the National Electrical Safety Code (NESC).
  - _Solution Statement_: Perform the work necessary to correct line clearance deficiencies that are identified on BGE's overhead distribution system.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains safety and compliance by correcting line clearance deficiencies on the overhead distribution system to meet BGE's Overhead Construction Standards and NESC requirements.
- **60504: Damages-Underground, Outdoor Lighting and Customer Requests *** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$12,590,840
  - _Problem Statement_: Underground or outdoor lighting equipment is damaged, or a customer is requesting to relocate BGE equipment.
  - _Solution Statement_: Damaged equipment is replaced, or the customer's relocation request is fulfilled.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by replacing damaged underground or outdoor lighting equipment or fulfilling customer requests to relocate BGE equipment.
- **60505: Distribution Equipment Replacement - Emergent** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$43,368,851
  - _Problem Statement_: Distribution equipment is defective or damaged and identified as beyond repair by first responder calls. Examples of first responders are customers, employees, municipalities, and emergency personnel.
  - _Solution Statement_: Defective or damaged distribution equipment is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project restores service and maintains reliability via replacement of defective or damaged distribution equipment identified as beyond repair through first responder calls.
- **61131: Buried Primary Cable Replacement- Emergent** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$5,693,842
  - _Problem Statement_: Buried primary cable fails and cannot be repaired, or a fault occurs on a buried primary cable that cannot be located.
  - _Solution Statement_: The large section of cable is replaced to restore service.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by replacing buried primary cable when it fails and cannot be repaired or when a fault cannot be located on primary cable.
- **61160: Pole Replacements - Planned** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$15,238,659
  - _Problem Statement_: This project represents non-emergent pole replacements. Poles are inspected every 10 years as required by COMAR 20.50.12.10. Poles identified for replacement through the inspection program are then assessed to determine if they are suitable for reinforcement to extend their life. A pole is disqualified for reinforcement because of a decayed pole top, large woodpecker holes, excessive cracking, and hazardous conditions. Non-emergent replacement of poles is planned and scheduled through construction.
  - _Solution Statement_: The defective pole is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports safe and planned replacement of defective poles identified through the inspection program when conditions such as decay, excessive cracking, woodpecker damage, or other hazards make reinforcement unsuitable. Defective poles are more likely to fall and create safety or reliability issues.
- **61161: Pole Replacements - Emergent** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$14,031,757
  - _Problem Statement_: A pole is identified as needing immediate replacement, through inspection or by first responder calls. Examples of first responders are customers, employees, municipalities, and emergency personnel.
  - _Solution Statement_: The defective pole that requires immediate attention is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project addresses poles identified through inspection or other means as needing immediate replacement by replacing the defective pole requiring immediate attention. Defective poles are more likely to fall and create safety or reliability issues.
- **61163: Emergency Response** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,258,807
  - _Problem Statement_: Outages or unsafe conditions are reported or identified.
  - _Solution Statement_: First responder restores service or mitigates unsafe conditions.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability and safety by restoring service or mitigating unsafe conditions when outages or hazards are reported or identified during normal operations.
- **61464: Damages - Overhead** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$11,352,522
  - _Problem Statement_: Overhead equipment is damaged by a third or unknown party and beyond repair.
  - _Solution Statement_: Damaged equipment is replaced.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by replacing overhead equipment that is damaged.
- **61523: Duct Line Cable Replacement - Emergent** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$16,174,891
  - _Problem Statement_: A duct line cable fails or has exceeded its useful life.
  - _Solution Statement_: Defective or end-of-life cable is removed from the duct line and replaced, or a section of cable is replaced in the manhole.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability by replacing defective or end-of-life duct line cable, either through full cable replacement in the duct line or sectional replacement in the manhole.

- **60802: Private Area Lighting Changes *** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$5,466,387
  - _Problem Statement_: A customer, municipal agency, or incorporated association of local residents request the changeout or conversion of company-owned, unmetered, overhead, or underground-supplied private area lighting.
  - _Solution Statement_: The private area light is changed out or converted pursuant to the Retail Electric Service Tariff Schedule PL.

  - _Problem Statement_: Additional BGE cable may be necessary to supply a customer-owned streetlight.
  - _Solution Statement_: Cable is installed to supply a customer-owned streetlight pursuant to the Retail Electric Service Tariff Schedule SL.

- **61090: Smart Lighting** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,418,015
  - _Problem Statement_: Existing customer streetlights are not enabled for modern technologies, which provide efficiencies and reliability benefits.
  - _Solution Statement_: Smart lighting and associated controls are deployed on existing customer-owned streetlights pursuant to the provisions for smart lighting included in the Retail Electric Service Tariff Schedule SL.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Deployment of smart controls will enable additional data and functionality for outage maps. It also enables quicker identification of and response to outages. It also reduces volume of customer calls to the call center (lower cost and increased customer satisfaction). Deployment of dimming controls reduce light pollution and trespassing. It also enables the customer to request on/off times.

**Category: System Performance - Distribution** — Category Total: \$167,252,494

- **54928: West Hamilton Substation 4521 and 4527 4kV Conversion** — In-Service Date: October 2024 — Amount: \$5,879,313
  - _Problem Statement_: The West Hamilton Substation 4521 and 4527 4kV feeders currently have aging substation and distribution assets, limited load capacity, and limited feeder restoration options. 4kV customers are served on infrastructure that is past the end of its useful life and rely on obsolete equipment for which replacements are not readily available, creating reliability challenges. When a fault occurs on a 4kV circuit mainline, significant time and resources are needed to restore service. The 4kV circuits lack system ties and automated switching typical of BGE's modern 13kV system and require manual switching, which increases restoration times for the customers they serve.
  - _Solution Statement_: The West Hamilton Substation 4521 and 4527 4kV Conversion project is part of an overall strategy to improve system performance for 4kV customers. The project converts the feeders from 4kV to 13kV, renews aging infrastructure, provides higher load capacity, and improves restoration capability. Conversion work also includes adding sectionalization and automated restoration equipment, upgrading conductors and obsolete substation and distribution equipment (e.g., transformers, switches), and adding restoration ties to modernize the system and improve operational flexibility.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves reliability and restoration performance for customers served by aging 4kV infrastructure by converting the feeders to 13kV, modernizing obsolete equipment, increasing load capacity, and adding automated restoration capability. It also reduces future maintenance and outage-related costs, supports better outage response, and provides more reliable service to customers in a densely loaded area with recurring seasonal overload issues.
- **55782: Monument Street Substation 4127 4kV Conversion** — In-Service Date: March 2024 — Amount: \$5,911,916
  - _Problem Statement_: The Monument Street Substation 4127 4kV feeder currently has aging substation and distribution assets, limited load capacity, and limited feeder restoration options. 4kV customers are served on infrastructure that is past the end of its useful life and rely on obsolete equipment for which replacements are not readily available, creating reliability challenges. It also reduces future maintenance and outage-related costs, supports better outage response, and provides more reliable service to customers in a densely loaded area with recurring seasonal overload issues.

  - _Problem Statement_: The Clifton Park Substation 4829 and 4834 4kV feeders currently have aging substation and distribution assets, limited load capacity, and limited feeder restoration options. 4kV customers are served on infrastructure that is past the end of its useful life and rely on obsolete equipment for which replacements are not readily available, creating reliability challenges. - **60808: Pad Mounted Distribution Automation Reclosers and Sectionalization** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$11,719,589
  - _Problem Statement_: The BGE underground system has minimal automation to sectionalize faults and automatically restore the non-faulted customers, which can cause excess customer outages and longer duration of the outages due to required repairs. This program funds the installation of devices to allow automation to be deployed on the BGE underground system.
  - _Solution Statement_: Locations are identified on the underground system where distribution automation and sectionalization can support improved reliability performance. Pad mounted distribution automation equipment is installed at these select locations to reduce the customer count per segment and limit the outage impact to only those customers directly affected. In addition to the installation of standalone distribution automation equipment, existing switchgear are considered for retrofit to add flexibility and remote-control functionality.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project enables customers on the BGE underground system to benefit from distribution automation and sectionalization by reducing outage impact to customers and supporting improved reliability performance.
- **61130: Targeted Reliability Improvement Program-System Performance** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,661,262
  - _Problem Statement_: COMAR 20.50.12.03 requires annual reporting of the poorest performing feeders on the BGE system. In addition to annual reporting, BGE is required to conduct reliability improvement work to ensure a poorest performing feeder is not listed for three consecutive years. The program funds system enhancement or configurations to improve reliability performance to avoid being listed on the poorest performing feeder list for three consecutive years.
  - _Solution Statement_: This program targets the poorest performing feeders on the BGE system. On a case-by-case basis, circuits are evaluated to determine possible actions to improve circuit reliability performance. Typical examples of projects deployed include the installation of distribution automation equipment, feeder reconfiguration, selective undergrounding, additional fusing and sectionalizing, enhanced vegetation management, and inspections.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This program complies with COMAR 20.50.12.03 and improves reliability performance at a feeder level by targeting the poorest performing feeders on the BGE system. Projects deployed and executed in support of this program improve customer reliability by decreasing the number of customer interruptions on the worst performing feeders on the BGE system.
- **61132: Cable Replacement Planned Primary** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$29,365,627
  - _Problem Statement_: As underground primary cable reaches its end of life, failure rates increase, resulting in more frequent customer outages and reduced reliability, and additional costs associated with repair. Planned cable replacements are a meaningful part of BGE's reliability program o achieve the Commission-approved reliability standards.
  - _Solution Statement_: This program replaces poor performing underground primary cable to reduce cable failure rates and improve system performance. When a cable section is identified as being in poor condition, the location is referred to the Planned Cable Replacement program, where the cable is evaluated and the scope of replacement is engineered. Jobs are prioritized based on factors such as cable type, number of failures, number of customers affected, critical customers affected, and cost.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This program enables improved system reliability and performance by reducing SAIFI, CAIDI, and CEMI through the replacement of aging infrastructure with more modern assets, resulting in a more robust underground system.
- **61141: Multiple Device Activations (MDA) Program - PSC** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,840,176
  - _Problem Statement_: COMAR 20.50.12.04 requires the reporting and reliability improvement of protective devices which operate five or more activations in a 12-month period. After the device is reported on the MDA list in a compliance period, the utility has 12 months to perform remediation work. After the remediation period of evaluation and repair, the device shall not experience five or more activations during either of the two subsequent 12-month reporting periods.
  - _Solution Statement_: The MDA program is designed to address devices that are reported or at risk of being reported to the Maryland PSC for failing to comply with the standard. A device identified as MDA will be evaluated to determine the cause of the performance issue. If the outage causes are related to vegetation, the remediation work would likely include targeted tree trimming, undergrounding a portion of the feeder, or installing Hendrix cable to reduce overhead exposure. Other remediation work may include installing equipment like fuses, reclosers, electronic resettable fuses or reconfiguring the infrastructure to limit customer exposure. The remediation work is situationally and locationally specific.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This program improves reliability and restoration performance by reducing the number of outages experienced by customers that have previously been impacted by repeated outages and helps enable compliance with COMAR 20.50.12.04.
- **61144: System Hardening** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$5,601,116
  - _Problem Statement_: The System Hardening program investigates targeted resiliency and reliability issues and addresses them through infrastructure changes. Overhead devices and circuits are selected for engineering by calculating a 3-year weighted average of interruptions by device. Then the projects are prioritized with devices with the most interruptions being the higher priority projects.
  - _Solution Statement_: The resiliency and reliability improvement work performed under this program includes, but is not limited to, installation of distribution automation equipment, feeder reconfiguration, selective undergrounding, Hendrix cable, additional fusing and sectionalizing, and enhanced vegetation management.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Customers benefit from a more robust system that contains auto restoration capability, which limits the number of customers affected for overhead outages and reduces the number of overhead outages in general.
- **61147: Overhead Distribution Automation Reclosers and Sectionalization** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$11,422,246
  - _Problem Statement_: When a fault occurs on the BGE electric system, all customers on the segment and downstream are interrupted by the fault. Crews must be deployed to correct a fault, in the absence of distribution automation equipment, which results in longer duration outages for customers and costs associated with deploying crews to address outages manually.
  - _Solution Statement_: Locations are identified on the overhead system where distribution automation and sectionalization equipment can be installed to support improved reliability performance. Pole mount distribution automation equipment is installed at these select locations to reduce the customer count per segment and limit the outage impact to only those customers directly affected as well as limiting the duration of the outage.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This program improves reliability by reducing the number of customers affected by faults and limits outage duration impact through the installation of automation and sectionalizing equipment, while enabling restoration without the need for crews to operate switches.
- **61159: Customer Reliability Support Projects** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$10,076,176
  - _Problem Statement_: As the existing electric infrastructure reaches its end of life and/or new technologies are deployed by customers, momentary or sustained interruptions may occur more frequently. This program is designed to identify system issues and upgrades to support system reliability.
  - _Solution Statement_: Engineers and technicians investigate reliability issues and create a work plan to address those issues. The work conducted under this program consists of proactive and reactive work. Various options for reliability support are considered.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This program supports reliability and customer satisfaction. Implementing engineering solutions improves system performance by reducing the frequency and duration of outages experienced by customers.
- **61435: Customers Experiencing Multiple Interruptions (CEMI) Program** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,541,367
  - _Problem Statement_: Experiencing multiple interruptions to customer's electric service in a short period negatively impacts the customer experience and is a reliability concern. Additionally, BGE is required under COMAR 20.50.12.05 to report certain CEMI statistics, which is used to determine which feeders warrant further evaluation.
  - _Solution Statement_: Engineers analyze the outage data and determine which customers fall under the multiple interruptions criteria (CEMI4/CEMI7). Based on circuit reliability issues that drive the CEMI performance, system upgrades and reconfigurations are considered and implemented to improve reliability performance for the impacted customers.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Engineering solutions are implemented for customers experiencing repeat interruptions, which reduce the duration and frequency of outages. Once engineering solutions have been implemented, it is rare that the same customers experience multiple outages within a given reporting cycle.
- **63111: Customer Resupply** — In-Service Date: September 2025 — Amount: \$3,672,962
  - _Problem Statement_: The retirement and re-supply of the feeders for this 34kV customer is an essential prerequisite towards the future retirement of the Riverside Substation which is currently a major aging infrastructure and environmental concern.
  - _Solution Statement_: Resupply customer via another substation.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports the future retirement and decommissioning of Riverside Substation by resupplying the customer via another substation which will reduce O&M maintenance costs associated with the substation and will also improve customer reliability by increasing the Polyseal feeder ties from 1 to 4 and removing outmoded infrastructure.
- **65143: Calverton Road Substation Feeder 4807 4kV Conversion** — In-Service Date: August 2025 — Amount: \$3,367,134
  - _Problem Statement_: The Calverton Road Substation Feeder 4807 4kV feeder currently has aging substation and distribution assets, limited load capacity, and limited feeder restoration options. 4kV customers are served on infrastructure that is past the end of its useful life and rely on obsolete equipment for which replacements are not readily available, creating reliability challenges. When a fault occurs on a 4kV circuit mainline, significant time and resources are needed to restore service. The project converts the feeder from 4kV to 13kV, renews aging infrastructure, provides higher load capacity, and improves restoration capability. Conversion work also includes adding sectionalization and automated restoration equipment, upgrading conductors and obsolete substation and distribution equipment (e.g., transformers, switches), and adding restoration ties to modernize the system and improve operational flexibility.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves reliability and restoration performance for customers served by aging 4kV infrastructure by converting the feeders to 13kV, modernizing obsolete equipment, increasing load capacity, and adding automated restoration capability. It also reduces future maintenance and outage-related costs, supports better outage response, and provides more reliable service to customers in a densely loaded area with recurring seasonal overload issues.

  - _Problem Statement_: The Clifton Park Substation 4824 4kV feeder currently has aging substation and distribution assets, limited load capacity, and limited feeder restoration options. 4kV customers are served on infrastructure that is past the end of its useful life and rely on obsolete equipment for which replacements are not readily available, creating reliability challenges. - **65213: Clifton Park Substation Feeders 4826 and 4828 4kV Conversion** — In-Service Date: December 2025 — Amount: \$5,249,290
  - _Problem Statement_: The Clifton Park Substation 4826 and 4828 4kV feeders currently have aging substation and distribution assets, limited load capacity, and limited feeder restoration options. 4kV customers are served on infrastructure that is past the end of its useful life and rely on obsolete equipment for which replacements are not readily available, creating reliability challenges. It also reduces future maintenance and outage-related costs, supports better outage response, and provides more reliable service to customers in a densely loaded area with recurring seasonal overload issues.
- **76080: Stoney Creek Submarine Cable Replacement** — In-Service Date: April 2024 — Amount: \$6,081,785
  - _Problem Statement_: In 2018, one of the ties with Riviera Beach Feeder 7316 failed. In the event of the Riviera Beach integral substation failure, customers on this circuit would be out of service until the integral substation is replaced. The integral substation has no mobile connection.
  - _Solution Statement_: Replace the failed cable and the old cable crossing Stoney Creek. Add additional duct to protect against the future failure of the existing cable.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves reliability and resiliency for Riviera Beach Feeder 7316 by replacing failed and aging cable infrastructure that currently exposes approximately 1,500 customers to the risk of an extended outage. It also increases tie and crossing capacity through added duct space, reducing outage risk and strengthening future operational flexibility.
- **77119: 34kV Extension to Long Reach Substation with Auto Transfer** — In-Service Date: November 2024 — Amount: \$7,931,706
  - _Problem Statement_: Currently there is only one supply line feeding Long Reach Substation, which creates a single point of failure along the supply. Existing motor operated switches used to supply the station are obsolete and have exceeded their useful life.
  - _Solution Statement_: Support the reliability and operability of Long Reach Substation by extending a second 34kV supply to the substation, installing auto-transfer capability on the high side of the 33-1 transformer, and removing existing obsolete motor operated switch mechanisms.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports reliability and resiliency at Long Reach Substation by adding a second 34kV supply and high-side auto-transfer capability, eliminating a single point of failure and reducing the risk of unplanned customer interruptions. It also replaces obsolete equipment, improves operational clarity, and reduces future maintenance, repair, and outage restoration costs.
- **78642: Customer Reliability Support Projects - Small Projects** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$5,717,472
  - _Problem Statement_: As electric infrastructure reaches the end of its useful life and new technologies are deployed by customers, momentary or sustained interruptions may occur more frequently, creating the need for smaller projects and upgrades to address system issues and improve reliability.
  - _Solution Statement_: Regional Distribution Engineering engineers and technicians investigate reliability issues, evaluate improvement options, and develop work plans to address smaller proactive and reactive reliability needs.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports smaller reliability projects that enhance electric system reliability by addressing identified issues through targeted engineering and design work from both internal and external sources.

**Category: System Performance - Substation** — Category Total: \$105,997,451

- **61267: Distribution Substation Spare Transformers** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$4,977,954
  - _Problem Statement_: Not having an adequate number of spare distribution substation transformers can lead to extended outage durations in the event of transformer failures. Absent a spare, distribution substation transformers can have lead time of over 12 months for replacements.
  - _Solution Statement_: To adhere to Distribution Planning requirements, maintain an adequate number of spare distribution substation transformers to react to historical levels of transformer failures.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports reliability and service consistency by maintaining an adequate number of spare distribution substation transformers to reduce outage duration and minimize time operating outside normal system configuration.
- **61349: Distribution Substation Transformer Pit Upgrades** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$8,893,750
  - _Problem Statement_: Distribution substation transformer secondary oil containment pits are identified as needing upgrades through inspection and testing. Upgrades are needed to comply with Section 311 of the Federal Water Pollution Control Act (aka the Clean Water Act) as well as U.S. Code of Federal Regulations, Title 40, Chapter I - EPA, Subchapter D, Part 112.
  - _Solution Statement_: Upgrade distribution substation transformer secondary containment pits that are in need of upgrades due to age or condition.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves environmental protection, safety, and compliance while reducing cleanup and restoration costs by upgrading aging substation transformer oil containment pits to minimize the risk and impact of oil releases.
- **61365: Distribution Substation Fire Protection Upgrades** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$4,030,666
  - _Problem Statement_: Fire protection at BGE's distribution substations needs improvement because some older equipment, while compliant with the standards in place at the time of installation, no longer meets current fire protection standards, creating safety and equipment concerns during a fire event.
  - _Solution Statement_: Manage fire risk by incorporating fire protections during the design, construction, and operation of the Company's distribution facilities.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project mitigates fire hazards, customer impact, and equipment damage through fire risk management techniques. It also helps prevent fires from starting and rapidly detects and controls fires that do occur to increase safety.
- **61377: Distribution Substation Security** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$56,120,784
  - _Problem Statement_: In light of increasing threats to damage electric infrastructure presented by bad actors, proper security steps need to be implemented to continue to protect critical substation facilities to prevent damaged and inoperable assets in the event of physical attack or intrusion. Improvements to the physical security of BGE's distribution substations ensure robust barriers are in place to protect critical assets and the public, while also maintaining reliability of the system. In addition to serving larger numbers of customers, BGE's distribution substations provide service to critical infrastructures such as pumping stations, hospitals, federal, state, and local government agencies, and military installations.
  - _Solution Statement_: Upgrade the physical security of certain BGE distribution substations using a tiered approach, leveraging best practices identified through industry review. The tiered approach assesses the criticality of the substations, including their role in public safety or security. Tier I security is generally reserved for assets that, should they be disrupted, would cause significant risks to public health and safety. Tier II electric distribution assets are moderately critical to the conservation of critical load areas or customers. Tier III electric distribution assets are important to the conservation of critical load areas or customers. Tier IV electric distribution assets are those determined not to be Tier I, II, or III.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Securing BGE's critical assets in light of growing and evolving security threats help BGE ensure continued safe and reliable delivery of electricity to our customers, which is critical to public safety and wellbeing and the economy of the State.
- **63038: Proactive Distribution Substation Transformer Replacement** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$7,492,431
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE's distribution substation transformers are aging and more susceptible to failure, requiring more costly repairs. Failures could occur and negatively impact reliability if not proactively replaced.
  - _Solution Statement_: Prioritize replacements based on age and other conditions that indicate a higher risk of failures.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Reduces the risk of in-service failure and extended customer outages while lowering costs and resource demands associated with unplanned repairs, including mobile transformer deployment.
- **67883: Distribution Substation Oil Circuit Breaker Replacements** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,556,737
  - _Problem Statement_: There are oil circuit breakers remaining in distribution substations that are costly to maintain, could cause oil leaks and damage to the environment, or fail due to age, which could impact safety or reliability. Parts for these aging oil circuit breakers are also becoming obsolete and harder to acquire.
  - _Solution Statement_: Replace oil circuit breakers in distribution substations with vacuum-based circuit breakers.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports reliability, avoids potential breaker mis-operations such as stuck-breaker condition, as well as avoids potential safety and environmental impacts due to failures. Benefits also include environmental benefits, employee safety, as well as avoided remedial costs, regulatory fines, and costs associated with the potential damage to adjacent equipment during a failure.
- **82826: Distribution Substation Motor Operated Safety Gates Installation** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,585,465
  - _Problem Statement_: Certain existing substation manual slide gates are heavy, difficult to operate, and not operable by a single-person crew, creating potential safety risks during gate operations and delaying substation access. Inability to access and operate substations quickly can lead to prolonged response times and extended reliability impacts for customers.
  - _Solution Statement_: Replace the manual slide gates with motorized gates. Standards have been updated to use motorized gates to address safety concerns ensure timely access to the substation when required.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project has the potential to reduce injuries and vehicular accidents during gate operations, helping to minimize work stoppages and productivity impacts, while supporting timely emergency response and routine operations that support customer reliability.
- **262388: Pinehurst Substation Aging Infrastructure Replacement** — In-Service Date: November 2025 — Amount: \$3,498,692
  - _Problem Statement_: Replacement of substation equipment including lightning arresters, Coupling Capacitor Voltage Transformer (CCVTs), breakers, station service transformers, relays, and circuit switchers is required due to condition and age to prevent reliability impacts.
  - _Solution Statement_: Replace aging equipment to prevent failures and reliability impacts.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability and reduces maintenance costs by replacing aging substation equipment that is susceptible to failure.
- _Total Projects < Threshold[^jcf7-fn2]_ — Amount: \$7,840,972

- **78798: Fleet Procurement-Internal Combustion Engine (ICE) Heavy Duty/Equip** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$10,232,846
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE needs to replace ICE Heavy Duty fleet assets that have reached the end of their life.
  - _Solution Statement_: BGE fleet evaluates Internal Combustion Engine (ICE) asset performance to identify the appropriate replacement. An asset's life cycle is determined by the age and condition of the vehicle.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Replacement of aging fleet assets improves vehicle/equipment reliability and reduces downtime. Reliable equipment ensures that BGE is able to safely and reliably operate and maintain the Gas and Electric Distribution systems.

  - _Problem Statement_: BGE needs to replace JEMS Heavy Duty fleet assets that have reached the end of their life.
  - _Solution Statement_: BGE fleet evaluates Jobsite Energy Management System asset performance to identify the appropriate replacement. An asset's life cycle is determined by the age and condition of the vehicle.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Replacement of aging fleet assets improves vehicle/equipment reliability and reduces downtime. Reliable equipment ensures that BGE is able to safely and reliably operate and maintain the Gas and Electric Distribution systems.
- **78804: Fleet Procurement-Internal Combustion Engine (ICE) Light Duty** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$11,284,644
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE needs to replace ICE Light Duty fleet assets that have reached the end of their life.
  - _Solution Statement_: BGE fleet evaluates Internal Combustion Engine (ICE) asset performance to identify the appropriate replacement. An asset's life cycle is determined by the age and condition of the vehicle.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Replacement of aging fleet assets improves vehicle/equipment reliability and reduces downtime. Reliable equipment ensures that BGE is able to safely and reliably operate and maintain the Gas and Electric Distribution systems.
- **78806: Fleet Procurement-Jobsite Energy Management System (JEMS) Light Duty** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,413,782
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE needs to replace fleet JEMS Light Duty assets that have reached the end of their life.
  - _Solution Statement_: BGE fleet evaluates Jobsite Energy Management System asset performance to identify the appropriate replacement. An asset's lifecycle is determined by the age and condition of the vehicle.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Replacement of aging fleet assets improves vehicle/equipment reliability and reduces downtime. Reliable equipment ensures that BGE is able to safely and reliably operate and maintain the Gas and Electric Distribution systems.
- **78807: Fleet Procurement-Battery Electric Vehicle (BEV) Light Duty** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,411,463
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE needs to replace fleet BEV Light Duty assets that have reached the end of their life.
  - _Solution Statement_: BGE fleet evaluates BEV Light Duty asset performance to identify the appropriate replacement. An asset's lifecycle is determined by the age and condition of the vehicle. The category also contains requests for new electric vehicles, which are driven by business needs.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Replacement of aging fleet assets improves vehicle/equipment reliability and reduces downtime. Reliable equipment ensures that BGE is able to safely and reliably operate and maintain the Gas and Electric Distribution systems.

- **60103: Overhead Distribution Tools Capital** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$6,519,459
  - _Problem Statement_: New and replacement tools are necessary to operate and maintain the overhead distribution system.
  - _Solution Statement_: This project is for the purchase of personal protective equipment (PPE), tools, safety equipment, and instruments that cost greater than $500 per item for the employees in the overhead distribution department. These tools are provided to distribution field personnel.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Provides the overhead distribution field personnel with the proper tools to do their jobs safely and effectively.
