### Witness Cloyd – sponsored categories

**Category: Business Services Company** — Category Total: \$33,441,528

- **61453: IT BSC - Capital** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$5,283,649
  - _Problem Statement_: There is a need for initial development and lifecycle upgrades of corporate systems and security that support BGE.
  - _Solution Statement_: Develop and upgrade corporate systems and facilities, such as Exelon Now (employee IT self-help and help desk ticket development portal) and middleware and encryption systems. Renew licenses for enterprise-wide systems.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Up-to-date and functional IT systems are necessary for BGE personnel to effectively perform their job functions. Routine updates and replacements are intended to maintain the health of current systems over the established lifespan.
- **74744: Project Apollo** — In-Service Date: January 2026 — Amount: \$27,867,740
  - _Problem Statement_: Exelon's financial and work planning and tracking systems have become increasingly aged, complex, and less efficient over time as the business has expanded and upgrades were delayed to focus on merger integration and other priorities. Many key systems, and the underlying hardware and databases, are no longer supported by vendors, creating additional costs and risks. Some higher risk components have the ability to negatively impact lower risk areas of the end-to-end architecture.
  - _Solution Statement_: Replace Exelon's legacy financial and work planning and tracking systems with a modernized back-office financial system that integrates a number of complex applications and systems interfaced into one scalable financial technology platform. Project Apollo will transform Exelon's ability to conduct its operations and serve customers by empowering the workforce through leading and simplified business processes, a more integrated systems platform, and an enhanced employee/user experience.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves financial system reliability, efficiency, and cost performance by implementing a cloud-based integrated financial system that streamlines processes, enhances data transparency, and reduces manual work.
- _Total Projects < Threshold[^jcf7-fn2]_ — Amount: \$290,139

**Category: Information Technology** — Category Total: \$172,511,069

- **59709: Land Mobile Radio (LMR)** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$25,453,130
  - _Problem Statement_: Aging communications equipment has created reliability and support challenges that could affect field operations and emergency response activities.
  - _Solution Statement_: Implement infrastructure enhancements and execute phased deployment activities to sustain reliable communications capabilities and support ongoing operational performance. The project modernizes aging assets through upgraded radio systems, enhanced tower infrastructure, advanced dispatch consoles, and deployment of subscriber equipment.
  - _Benefits Statement_: The investment strengthens the reliability and resiliency of critical field communications, enabling more effective coordination between the Operations Control Center and field crews during routine operations, outage restoration, and emergency response. By upgrading the aging infrastructure, the project improves system availability, reduces operational risk, and supports faster, more dependable service restoration. The enhanced platform also increases operational flexibility, allowing crews to efficiently operate across service territories as needed, ultimately minimizing disruption risk and avoiding extended restoration times for customers.
- **60727: Equipment Refresh - Capital IT** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,223,580
  - _Problem Statement_: Each year, various PCs, servers, and network equipment are determined to be at end of sustainable life, expiration of warranty, or experience frequent failures due to aged hardware and software. Equipment in operation that is beyond its end-of-life creates operational risks to the BGE environment and heightens cybersecurity risks. Impacted units need to be replaced.
  - _Solution Statement_: PCs, servers, and network equipment are refreshed/replaced as necessary.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Up-to-date and functional IT equipment is necessary for BGE personnel to effectively perform their job functions. Routine equipment replacements are intended to maintain the health of aging components that have an established lifespan.
- **64601: Line Sensors Deployment Support** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,061,997
  - _Problem Statement_: Limited visibility into distribution system issues increases the time required to identify faults and respond to outages.
  - _Solution Statement_: Implement line sensor capabilities through the deployment, configuration, and integration of advanced sensing devices and supporting communications infrastructure. The effort includes network connectivity enhancements and end-to-end provisioning activities to enable improved distribution system monitoring, faster fault detection and localization, and more responsive outage management.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Improves visibility into distribution system conditions, enabling faster identification of faults and more effective outage response and restoration efforts. Reduces time spent locating issues and enhances coordination of field activities, contributing to more efficient operations, improved system reliability, and a better overall customer experience.
- **64741: Exelon Utilities (EU) Core Geographic Information System Implementation** — In-Service Date: November 2025 — Amount: \$16,904,224
  - _Problem Statement_: Exelon's operating companies, including BGE, rely on disparate GIS systems, interfaces, and manual processes that limit data quality, standardization, and accessibility. As the authoritative source of network topology and asset data, GIS underpins critical platforms including ADMS and EAM which require accurate, timely geospatial data to operate effectively. It also reduces efficiency in planning, operations, and mutual assistance due to inconsistent data and lack of a common network model. Without a unified GIS platform and standardized processes, dependent systems risk misaligned data, reduced situational awareness, and challenges managing increasing grid complexity, including digital energy resources (DER) integration.
  - _Solution Statement_: Deploy the Core GIS program which delivers an integrated, modernized geospatial platform by implementing the updated GE Smallworld application across electric and gas operations, supported by standardized asset design, as-built (capturing the actual field-installed configuration of assets to ensure GIS reflects real-world conditions), and data maintenance processes and a unified data model. This enables consistent, high-quality network data and seamless integration with critical systems, including ADMS, to support more efficient, coordinated, and data-driven utility operations, including the accurate modeling and integration of DERs.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Enhanced data accuracy, system integration, and platform usability support overall network reliability and improve customer satisfaction. The implementation of a common GIS data model, combined with strengthened data quality controls and the digitization of legacy data into a centralized platform, increases confidence in network information and reduces the risk of configuration errors. Modernized system integration enables more timely and consistent data sharing across critical systems, improving operational coordination, workforce safety and response to distribution events, while supporting more effective DER integration, visibility, and management and driving more efficient planning, execution, and restoration activities.
- **76887: BGE Customer Care & Billing (CC&B) Upgrade 2.8** — In-Service Date: February 2024 — Amount: \$3,547,722
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE's current customer care/billing system went out of vendor support in 2023, requiring upgrade to an enhanced platform in order to avoid increased support costs, degraded capabilities, and potential security risks.
  - _Solution Statement_: The CC&B 2.8 Upgrade Project implements a phased and disciplined approach to modernizing BGE's customer billing platform by upgrading to a fully supported version, stabilizing operations post-deployment, and enhancing user readiness. The project leverages shared enterprise resources to improve efficiency while ensuring thorough system validation. Critically, the upgrade establishes the necessary foundation for a future transition to a shared, cloud-based environment, aligning with Exelon's enterprise modernization strategy and enabling a more secure, reliable, and scalable platform to support customer care and billing operations.
  - _Benefits Statement_: BGE CC&B 2.8 Upgrade Project delivers a secure, reliable, and future-ready customer billing platform by maintaining system supportability, cybersecurity and advancing enterprise alignment to a shared cloud strategy, while minimizing customer disruption through a phased, stable transition.
- **78077: Distributed Energy Resource Management System (DERMS) Implementation** — In-Service Date: December 2025 — Amount: \$8,065,282
  - _Problem Statement_: The transformation required to meet the 2022 Maryland Climate Solution Now Act (CSNA), the 2023 Maryland Energy Storage Targets/Program legislation, and to be prepared for FERC Order No. 2222 implementation by the original required date of 02/02/2026 is driving the need for BGE to deploy a Distributed Energy Resource Management System (DERMS). The current solution for monitoring and controlling BGE's DER assets involves a custom real time calculation in the Distribution Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition (DSCADA) system which requires manual maintenance and is not scalable to meet the DER growth expected in the near future.
  - _Solution Statement_: Implement Open Systems International (OSI) Intergra DERMS as an out of box standalone solution with minimal customizations. The deployment of the BGE DERMS is a solution to prepare BGE operations for managing DERs in support of state policy/legislation. The OSI Intergra solution will provide advanced capabilities for managing the Battery Energy Storage systems (BESS) and future DER assets. This solution will also enable BGE to scale up additional DER assets on to the distribution system over the next several years.
  - _Benefits Statement_: The OSI Intergra solution provides advanced capabilities for managing the BESS and future DER assets. Replacing the real-time calculation solution in DSCADA with OSI DERMS reduces future script maintenance risks. This solution also enables BGE to scale up additional DER assets on to the distribution system over the next several years, which will provide more reliable service and variety of energy delivery solutions to our customers. The implementation of this system is foundational for future DERMS capabilities such as flexible interconnect, VPPs, and market participation. The project supports BGE's ability to meet the Maryland DRIVE Act and Maryland Energy Storage Program.
- **78282: Exelon Utilities (EU) Outage Reporting & Analytics (ORA) Implementation** — In-Service Date: May 2024 — Amount: \$4,565,330
  - _Problem Statement_: FocalPoint, BGE's current Outage Communication System (OCS) is at end of life, the vendor ABB no longer provides software patches for the FocalPoint platform and is phasing out the platform. Additionally, BGE identified multiple technology challenges with FocalPoint that result in performance degradation during larger storm events and other widespread disruptions. Risk to the business and operations is that the OCS will encounter similar performance challenges with future storms. There is risk with having to maintain a system with limited vendor support and no options to upgrade to keep current.
  - _Solution Statement_: Implementation of a common outage reporting and analytics toolset to drive convergence across EU. Leverage the EU Data Analytics Platform to build a central repository for all outage data and build common outage reporting and analytics capabilities. The proposed technical solution will be tightly integrated with the advanced distribution management system (ADMS) program and the strategy for the Master Data Repository (MDR).
  - _Benefits Statement_: Consolidating legacy systems onto the Data Analytics Platform enhances performance, reduces latency, and strengthens reliability during major storm events. The platform enables more timely and consistent Estimated Time to Restoration (ETR) communications, stabilizes customer-facing systems, and reduces operational risk and cost by retiring legacy infrastructure and establishing common processes and governance. It also expands support capabilities through a scalable, cloud-based platform. A clear example of this resiliency occurred during the July 2024 global CrowdStrike outage, which rendered many Windows-based devices across Exelon unusable, including those in distribution control rooms. While legacy reporting systems were inaccessible, ORA remained available via mobile devices, allowing continued access to outage data and reporting. This demonstrated ORA's ability to maintain operational continuity and customer communications during a widespread disruption.
- **78348: Exelon Utilities (EU) Enterprise Asset Management (EAM) 2.0 - Asset Suite 8 Replacement** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$10,241,792
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE's current enterprise platforms for assets, preventative and corrective maintenance work, and supply chain operations are outdated and fragmented, creating operational inefficiencies and cybersecurity risks. The electric side relies on Asset Suite 8 (AS8), a system in use since 1998 and will no longer be supported by the vendor after 2029. These legacy systems lack scalability, and modern integration capabilities resulting in manual processes, poor data quality, and inconsistent billing accuracy. In addition, the current platforms cannot expand to support new asset types and ownership models, including distributed energy resources. Continued reliance on AS8 beyond its lifecycle increases risk exposure and maintenance costs, while limiting BGE's ability to deliver real-time updates, predictive maintenance, and improved customer experiences.
  - _Solution Statement_: The EAM 2.0 initiative delivers a modern, cloud-hosted enterprise asset management solution that replaces BGE's legacy AS8 platform and the standalone Gas Mobile Dispatch system with a single, unified environment. This scalable platform provides comprehensive, end to end asset lifecycle management and integrates seamlessly with key enterprise systems--including Apollo, GIS, One MDS, and multiple customer-facing applications, while also enabling advanced analytics capabilities. EAM 2.0 improves operational performance by automating manual workflows, enhancing data accuracy, and supporting the management of emerging asset classes, such as solar installations, battery systems, and EV charging infrastructure. The solution strengthens cybersecurity posture, reduces ongoing support and maintenance costs, and elevates customer experience through real time work status visibility, predictive maintenance functionality, and AI assisted resource optimization.
  - _Benefits Statement_: The EU-EAM 2.0 investment delivers significant enterprise value by establishing a secure, scalable digital backbone on the Industrial and Financial Systems (IFS) platform and deploying key capabilities through Planned Category Management (PCM) and Contract Lifecycle Management (CLM) releases. These implementations modernize work, asset, supply, and contract management by standardizing processes and data, improving visibility, and enabling stronger cross-functional collaboration, while automation and streamlined workflows reduce manual effort and cycle times. Expansion of the EAM platform to the full BGE user community strengthens operational planning and execution, supporting reliability, fewer outages, faster restoration, and better customer communication. The PCM and CLM releases further improve procurement and contract processes by enabling data-driven sourcing decisions, accelerating contract lifecycle activities, and strengthening governance, ultimately optimizing company spend and delivering more efficient, cost-effective service for customers.
- **79402: Exelon Utilities (EU) Customer Flight Path Program** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,124,946
  - _Problem Statement_: Current customer support and services are not aligned with rapidly evolving customer expectations for data-driven personalization, advanced payment options, and clarity/flexibility concerning the customer experience. Limitations in self-service options lead to increased call volume and high customer effort for resolution. The identified issue significantly impacts customers by hindering their service experience in several ways, including increased customer effort to complete transactions and participate in programs, including equitable access and offerings across multiple customer segments such as limited income to small/medium businesses.
  - _Solution Statement_: The Customer Flight Path Program advances BGE's online customer-facing capabilities by enhancing how customers access and manage information through BGE's website, mobile app, and IVR system. The program expands functionality across five product categories: Manage Energy Use, Outage, Sign Up and Move, Manage My Account, and Billing and Payment. Through these enhancements, BGE increases customer self-service options, expands payment choices, reduces customer effort during registration and service requests, improves outage communications for both planned and emergent events, supports more seamless access to energy assistance applications, increases access to energy choice options, and improves the digital experience for limited-income customers. The program also enables BGE to participate with other Exelon utilities in a coordinated, cost-effective build that enhanced customer-facing capabilities while supporting reliability and security, operational efficiency, and customer effort and satisfaction.
  - _Benefits Statement_: The Customer Flight Path Program enhances BGE's digital customer experience by giving customers more convenient, secure, and self-service options to manage billing, account information, energy usage, outages, and service requests. Billing enhancements, including "My Bill Insights," help customers better understand their bill and bill components, while targeted notifications for vulnerable customers provide clearer payment status information and help reduce disconnection risk at the time of payment. Start, stop, and move service enhancements reduce customer effort by helping customers identify and correct common self-service errors without needing to contact a Customer Service Representative, increase digital success rates, enable same-day service turn-on, and provide seamless handoffs from IVR to text-based self-service. Account management improvements, including multi-factor authentication for residential accounts, strengthen protection of customer data and account details. Energy management enhancements reduce friction for customers adopting distributed energy resources, such as solar and electric vehicles, through expedited automation and capacity planning improvements that help customers select and complete projects more successfully and easily. Overall, the program improves customer satisfaction, strengthens security, increases operational efficiency, and expands convenient access to key BGE services.
- **79404: Exelon Utilities (EU) Customer Flight Path: Large Customer Services (LCS) Program** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$3,311,496
  - _Problem Statement_: Large Commercial Customers who are evaluating moving into the utility territory have limited information available to make an informed decision. Often, they cannot easily evaluate key data, such as programs or load availability, to decide to move or invest further into the communities where they do business. The vast majority of online tools available to customers today are designed for residential customers, which limits large commercial customers' ability to seamlessly access billing information, analyze sustainability goal opportunities, or enroll in programs that could increase affordability and sustainability.
  - _Solution Statement_: Develop customer-facing online tools to provide large commercial and industrial customers and their account managers with centralized, self-service access to critical business information, including billing, energy usage, program enrollment, outage information, and notification management. These capabilities improve accessibility, streamlined account management, and better aligned digital services with the operational needs of this customer segment. Additionally, this investment is expected to support greater business proliferation and infrastructure expansion due to greater online tools being available for large commercial customers to evaluate the service territory for their business plans. This project is also expected to help large commercial customers to meet greenhouse gas emission standards through more informed decision making and increased participation in programs, supporting clean and efficient energy choice.
  - _Benefits Statement_: The EU Customer Flight Path: LCS Program investment creates dedicated digital experiences for BGE's medium and large commercial customers, allowing them to self‑serve for billing, usage, outage information, and program enrollment. The investment reduces reliance on manual support while improving transparency, efficiency, and customer satisfaction for large customers. Examples included program finder and enrollment tools and account management tool improvements designed to best support reduced customer effort and increased satisfaction for this customer segment.
- **85303: Mesh Network Expansion-BGE** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,254,563
  - _Problem Statement_: Legacy communications capabilities were insufficient to support rising operational demands and advanced monitoring needs, such as real-time visibility, increased telemetry, distribution automation, and enhanced event detection, limiting system performance and scalability as grid modernization expanded.
  - _Solution Statement_: Expand the communications network to support additional monitoring, improve system visibility, and strengthen operational performance.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Expansion of the communications network improves system visibility and enables faster identification and response to distribution system events, such as equipment failures, voltage fluctuations, switching operations, faults, and storm-related damage, reducing outage frequency and duration. Upgrades to wireless infrastructure and connectivity strengthen support for distribution automation and real-time grid monitoring. These improvements enable quicker restoration during outages and storms, more accurate and timely customer communications, and improved coordination of field crews, resulting in enhanced reliability, improved customer experience, and a more resilient distribution system.
- **261779: BGE Utility Consolidated Billing Community Solar - Capital** — In-Service Date: December 2025 — Amount: \$14,246,479
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE's current Customer Care and Billing (CC&B) environment does not support the data exchange, billing, crediting, and reporting capabilities required to implement Community Solar utility consolidated billing in compliance with 2023 Maryland Legislative Session House Bill 908. There is currently no interface between subscriber organizations and CC&B to exchange subscriber and billing data, which prevents BGE from applying Community Solar subscription charges directly on the customer bill, managing rollover and budget billing requirements, and meeting new Commission reporting obligations for billing and crediting errors. Without these system changes, BGE would be unable to comply with the January 1, 2026 statutory deadline and the expanded customer billing requirements established under HB908.
  - _Solution Statement_: The integrated Community Solar billing solution enhances the CC&B platform and establishes a secure interface with subscriber organizations through the Exelon Utilities Portal. This solution enables bi-directional data exchange for subscriber enrollment, billing determinants, and credit information, allowing Community Solar charges and credits to be calculated, applied, and displayed directly on the customer's BGE bill. The modified CC&B system supports utility consolidated billing, including net crediting, application of credits for standard and Budget Billing customers, management of rollover credits, and visibility of applied and banked credits. Additionally, the solution implements automated tracking and reporting capabilities to satisfy Commission requirements for billing and crediting error reporting, ensuring compliance with HB908 by the January 1, 2026 deadline.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This investment supports compliance with HB908 requirements by enabling structured tracking and reporting of billing and crediting activity while ensuring credits are applied within mandated timeframes. It improves the customer experience by integrating Community Solar directly into CC&B, allowing customers to pay subscriber organizations through their BGE bill without the need for separate billing arrangements. Centralizing Community Solar data through the Exelon Utilities Portal enhances data accuracy, visibility, and operational efficiency. In addition, billing transparency is strengthened by clearly displaying both applied and banked credits on customer bills, improving customer understanding and confidence.
- **262531: Windows 11 Mobile Dispatch Terminal (MDT) Software Refresh** — In-Service Date: December 2024 — Amount: \$4,255,024
  - _Problem Statement_: Aging technology in Mobile Dispatch Terminals (MDTs) is a risk to the performance of these devices during critical activities, such as during storm and restoration support. Also, the oneMDS platform has more rigid performance requirements for end devices that the MDTs cannot meet with outdated technology.
  - _Solution Statement_: Refresh the MDT technology to maintain current technology on these devices. This refresh project will reduce the risk of having outdated MDTs in use for critical activities. It also ensures MDTs are compatible with the technical requirements for the oneMDS platform.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Provides reliable, up-to-date MDTs that enable efficient operations, responsive field support, and, as a result, a better customer experience. Reduces security exposure and compliance risk by proactively replacing end-of-lifecycle MDTs before they require costly remediation; minimizes field disruption and downtime; and lowers unnecessary operating costs.
- _Total Projects < Threshold[^jcf7-fn2]_ — Amount: \$57,255,506

**Category: Real Estate and Facilities Management** — Category Total: \$56,082,496

- **66622: Office & Support Facilities (O&SF) Building Security Program** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$9,833,113
  - _Problem Statement_: Current levels of office building and support facility security are dated and require replacement to ensure the security of the sites and the safety of the personnel that work at those locations.
  - _Solution Statement_: The O&SF Building Security Program increases the security and safety at company office buildings and support facilities (i.e., service centers). The O&SF program was designed to reduce unauthorized access to personnel, equipment, systems, and materials utilizing a defense in depth strategy. The program uses a risk-based approach and tiering methodologies that analyze the criticality of each type of asset in order to prioritize investment. While the tier dictates the protections implemented at each site, the protections are designed for the threats within BGE's service territory.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Improved security reduces the likelihood that business continuity is disrupted by external threats.
- **76954: Electric Vehicle Infrastructure Program** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$5,077,182
  - _Problem Statement_: As BGE increases its fleet of electric vehicles (EV), there is a need to add additional charging stations and Jobsite Energy Management Systems (JEMS).
  - _Solution Statement_: Build electrification infrastructure at BGE facilities to keep up with the pace of EVs.
  - _Benefits Statement_: Charging infrastructure supports BGE's fleet of EV vehicles that support the safe and reliable operation of BGE's electric and gas systems. It also supports decarbonization initiatives that drive environmental benefits. This will also decrease fuel costs for our trucks.
- **86440: Cockeysville Pole Barn** — In-Service Date: August 2024 — Amount: \$3,300,558
  - _Problem Statement_: Cockeysville's existing, on-site storage facilities are aging and offer insufficient storage options for BGE's equipment. This facility is in need of several outdoor structures, including the pole barn.
  - _Solution Statement_: Build the structures for storing all the utility poles so weather does not impact overall pole condition. The barn also enhances storage efficiencies as the poles take up a lot of parking lot space. Some equipment for moving the poles sits in the barn.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves asset protection, operational efficiency, and cost performance by constructing covered storage for poles and equipment to reduce weather-related damage, extend service life, optimize site layout, and enhance security and storage capacity.
- **86468: Spring Gardens Operations Storage Facility (OSF) Building First Floor Renovation** — In-Service Date: September 2024 — Amount: \$4,669,307
  - _Problem Statement_: Spring Gardens OSF Building's First Floor contains unrenovated areas that require upgrades. Refresh of existing workspaces and conference rooms that are currently outdated and/or unusable are also needed.
  - _Solution Statement_: Update the existing unrenovated areas and workspaces with upgraded equipment and materials, including new furniture and technology. These renovations will improve the working environment for employees. Renovations are to include refreshed men's and women's locker rooms. An expanded Occupational Health Services suite will be added to accommodate employees that will feature new exam rooms, private offices, and a larger reception area.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves the working environment and accommodates staffing needs within the existing building footprint, reducing the need for additional real estate and improving space utilization, efficiency, and associated facility costs.
- **261872: Electric Operations Building (EOB) Chilled Water Piping Replacement** — In-Service Date: November 2025 — Amount: \$4,467,335
  - _Problem Statement_: The chilled water pipes are too small to provide full cooling capacity at EOB.
  - _Solution Statement_: Replace the chilled water piping with a larger diameter piping that enables the required cooling.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project improves reliability, safety, and efficiency of its building systems by replacing undersized chilled water piping to meet cooling requirements, enhance system control and monitoring, reduce maintenance costs, and minimize the risk of system failures and operational disruptions.
- _Total Projects < Threshold[^jcf7-fn2]_ — Amount: \$28,735,001

**Category: Other** — Category Total: \$98,490,679

- **60940: Distribution Transformer Purchases** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$52,409,908
  - _Problem Statement_: An adequate number of distribution transformers are needed when new installations or replacements are required.
  - _Solution Statement_: Purchase distribution transformers to ensure the necessary equipment is available when new installations or replacements are required.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project maintains reliability and service consistency by ensuring that BGE has an adequate number of distribution transformers available when new installations or replacements are required.
- **60957: Meter Purchase** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$10,561,851
  - _Problem Statement_: Residential, industrial, and commercial customers request new service and/or changes, which require the installation of electric meters.
  - _Solution Statement_: Purchase meters to ensure the necessary equipment is available for installation when residential, industrial, and commercial customers request new service and/or changes.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project ensures that BGE has the necessary equipment to provide electricity to customers when new services and/or changes are requested.
- **60975: Load Management Capital** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$7,792,074
  - _Problem Statement_: BGE needs to maintain load management programs to meet EmPOWER Maryland requirements. For 2024, BGE had a statutory EmPOWER MD MWh savings goal. In 2024, the Commission issued Implementation Order No. 91175 in connection with House Bill 864, which established greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions reduction goals in place of the 2024 MWh goals for 2025 and 2026 for the EmPOWER MD program.
  - _Solution Statement_: To support the achievement of both the MWh goals for 2024, and the GHG emissions reduction goals for 2025 and 2026, BGE continues to maintain its Load Management program, which encompasses the legacy PeakRewards program, as well as the Connected Rewards and Smart Energy Rewards programs. BGE's Load Management program promotes energy conservation amongst our customers and provides significant relief to the grid in times of emergency, which aligns with environmental sustainability goals to support Maryland's electric grid infrastructure. This project includes the non-IT capital related to BGE's suite of load management programs, which were approved by the Commission in Order No. 91461.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This project supports achievement of MWh savings, GHG emissions reduction goals, and regulatory compliance by maintaining Load Management programs that promote energy conservation, reduce grid strain during emergencies, lower operating costs, and simplify customer management and billing processes. Through innovation (as described in Commission Order No. 90957), the Load Management Programs seek to implement flexible strategies which optimize load on a daily, weekly, or monthly (year-round) basis, and provide locational demand response capabilities.
- **77112: BGE Grid Communications and Connectivity - Capital** — In-Service Date: Monthly / Various — Amount: \$12,283,341
  - _Problem Statement_: Fiber investments are needed to enhance BGE's ability to deploy advanced grid technologies and support modern utility operations. However, several BGE substations lack enhanced or upgraded fiber connectivity. The current gap restricts real-time monitoring, fault isolation, and integration of Distributed Energy Resources (DERs) such as solar and battery storage. It also impedes the delivery of high-speed broadband to surrounding communities, many of which face digital inequities. Without fiber, BGE cannot fully enable smart grid capabilities or support the growing demand for reliable, secure, and scalable communications infrastructure.
  - _Solution Statement_: Improve and expand fiber optic network to create a robust communications backbone, improving reliability, resiliency, safety, and enabling distributed energy resources (DERs) and renewables. This project includes the work for BGE's portion of the underground fiber project for which BGE submitted a grant application in September 2022 to the National Telecommunications and Information Administration under the Infrastructure and Investment Jobs Act. As BGE noted in its PC56 - Maryland Joint Exelon Utilities' Monthly IIJA Report filed with the Commission on November 3, 2025, effective October 30, 2025, BGE terminated the grant agreement with NIST and NTIA that the Company entered into with the federal government for the Middle Mile Grant Program.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This investment supports the high-bandwidth, low‑latency communications needed for utility operations and real‑time monitoring. It also enables advanced grid technologies such as distribution automation, improving reliability and resiliency. For customers, it will improve outage response times, expand smart energy program access, and support DER integration. The fiber network will increase access to affordable high‑speed broadband, promoting digital equity and economic development in nearby communities. This project aligns with Maryland's clean energy and digital equity goals.
- **262092: BGE Grid Communication and Connectivity (GCC) Fiber Route - Howard** — In-Service Date: December 2025 — Amount: \$6,534,146
  - _Problem Statement_: The substations on the Howard route need sufficient fiber connectivity to enhance BGE's ability to deploy advanced grid technologies and support modern utility operations. The current gap restricts real-time monitoring, fault isolation, and integration of Distributed Energy Resources (DERs), such as solar and battery storage. It also impedes the delivery of high-speed broadband to surrounding communities, many of which face digital inequities. Without fiber, BGE cannot fully enable smart grid capabilities or support the growing demand for reliable, secure, and scalable communications infrastructure.
  - _Solution Statement_: BGE's GCC program will fund the deployment of approximately 9.7 miles of fiber to connect substations along the Howard route into BGE's broader fiber ring topology. The program is intended to facilitate the fiber expansion and BGE's commitment to the IIJA Middle Mile Fiber Grant award. As BGE noted in its PC56 - Maryland Joint Exelon Utilities' Monthly IIJA Report filed with the Commission on November 3, 2025, effective October 30, 2025, BGE terminated the grant agreement with NIST and NTIA that the Company entered into with the federal government for the Middle Mile Grant Program.
  - _Benefits Statement_: This investment supports the high‑bandwidth, low‑latency communications needed for utility operations and real‑time monitoring. It also enables advanced grid technologies such as distribution automation, improving reliability and resiliency. For customers, it will improve outage response times, expand smart energy program access, and support DER integration. The fiber network will increase access to affordable high‑speed broadband, promoting digital equity and economic development in nearby communities. This project aligns with Maryland's clean energy and digital equity goals.
